68: static PREEMPTION_A_STARTED: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);
69: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
70: static PREEMPTION_B_STARTED: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);
71: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
72: static PREEMPTION_TEST_FAILED: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);
73: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
74: static PREEMPTION_FPU_FAILED: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);
75: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
76: const PREEMPTION_TEST_TICK_BUDGET: u64 = 8;
77: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
78: const PREEMPTION_PATTERN_A: [u8; 16] = [0xa1; 16];
79: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
80: const PREEMPTION_PATTERN_B: [u8; 16] = [0xb2; 16];
81: 
82: pub(crate) type ThreadEntry = extern "C" fn(*mut (), usize);
83: 
84: struct KernelThreadRuntime {
85:     policy: Scheduler<MAX_THREADS, QUEUE_COUNT, MAX_THREADS>,
86:     saved_rsp: [usize; CONTEXT_COUNT],
87:     fx_state: [FxSaveArea; CONTEXT_COUNT],
88:     thread_ids: [Option<ThreadId>; MAX_THREADS],
89:     stack_virtual_starts: [Option<u64>; MAX_THREADS],
90:     entries: [Option<ThreadEntry>; MAX_THREADS],
91:     arguments: [usize; MAX_THREADS],
92:     deadlines: [Option<u64>; MAX_THREADS],
93:     event_queue: WaitQueueId,
94:     #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
95:     process_exit_queue: WaitQueueId,
96:     #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
97:     process_start_queue: WaitQueueId,
98:     #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
99:     process_start_closed: bool,
100:     timer_queues: [WaitQueueId; MAX_THREADS],
101:     current_context: usize,
102:     input_events_enabled: bool,
103:     enable_thread_interrupts: bool,
104:     ticks: &'static AtomicTickCounter,
105: }
106: 
107: impl KernelThreadRuntime {
108:     fn new(ticks: &'static AtomicTickCounter) -> Result<Self, SchedulerError> {
109:         let mut policy = Scheduler::new();
110:         let event_queue = policy.create_wait_queue()?;
111:         let mut timer_queues = [event_queue; MAX_THREADS];
112:         for queue in &mut timer_queues {
113:             *queue = policy.create_wait_queue()?;
114:         }
115:         #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
116:         let process_exit_queue = policy.create_wait_queue()?;
117:         #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
118:         let process_start_queue = policy.create_wait_queue()?;
119:         Ok(Self {
120:             policy,
121:             saved_rsp: [0; CONTEXT_COUNT],
122:             fx_state: [FxSaveArea::INITIAL; CONTEXT_COUNT],
123:             thread_ids: [None; MAX_THREADS],
124:             stack_virtual_starts: [None; MAX_THREADS],
125:             entries: [None; MAX_THREADS],
126:             arguments: [0; MAX_THREADS],
127:             deadlines: [None; MAX_THREADS],
128:             event_queue,
129:             #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
130:             process_exit_queue,
131:             #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
132:             process_start_queue,
133:             #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
134:             process_start_closed: false,
135:             timer_queues,
136:             current_context: 0,
137:             input_events_enabled: false,
138:             enable_thread_interrupts: false,
139:             ticks,
140:         })
141:     }
142: 
143:     fn all_threads_exited(&self) -> bool {
144:         self.thread_ids
145:             .iter()
146:             .flatten()
147:             .all(|thread| self.policy.state(*thread) == Ok(ThreadState::Exited))
148:     }
149: 
150:     fn wake_expired(&mut self) {
151:         let now = self.ticks.ticks();
152:         for slot in 0..MAX_THREADS {
153:             if self.deadlines[slot].is_some_and(|deadline| now >= deadline) {
154:                 self.deadlines[slot] = None;
155:                 if self.policy.wake_one(self.timer_queues[slot]).is_err() {
156:                     // A scheduler that cannot wake a waiter leaks a permanently
157:                     // sleeping thread; stop loudly instead of limping.
158:                     crate::kprintln!("scheduler: timer wake failed for queue {}", slot);
159:                     halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure();
160:                 }
161:             }
162:         }
163:     }
164: 
165:     fn wake_ready_events(&mut self) -> bool {
166:         self.wake_expired();
167:         let input_event = self.input_events_enabled
168:             && rustix_kernel::input::INPUT_EVENT_PENDING.swap(false, Ordering::AcqRel);
169:         if input_event && self.policy.wake_all(self.event_queue).is_err() {
170:             // A scheduler that cannot wake its waiters leaks permanent blockage;
171:             // stop loudly instead of limping.
172:             crate::kprintln!("scheduler: event wake failed");
173:             halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure();
174:         }
175:         input_event
176:     }
177: 
178:     unsafe fn run_from_raw(runtime_pointer: *mut Self) {
179:         let initial_flags = disable_interrupts();
180:         if ACTIVE_RUNTIME
181:             .compare_exchange(
182:                 core::ptr::null_mut(),
183:                 runtime_pointer,
184:                 Ordering::Release,
185:                 Ordering::Relaxed,
186:             )
187:             .is_err()
188:         {
189:             halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure();
190:         }
191:         let first = {
192:             // SAFETY: The caller supplies the live, exclusively owned runtime;
193:             // the temporary reference ends before the context switch.
194:             let runtime = unsafe { &mut *runtime_pointer };
195:             match runtime.policy.dispatch() {
196:                 Ok(Some(thread)) => {
197:                     let context = thread.slot_index() + 1;
198:                     runtime.current_context = context;
199:                     Some((context, runtime.saved_rsp[context]))
200:                 }
201:                 Ok(None) => None,
202:                 Err(_) => {
203:                     THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
204:                     None
205:                 }
206:             }
207:         };
208:         if let Some((new_context, new_rsp)) = first {
209:             // SAFETY: Context zero is the active bootstrap stack; the selected
210:             // synthetic frame and both aligned FXSAVE areas remain live.
211:             unsafe {
212:                 rustix_arch_context_switch(
213:                     core::ptr::addr_of_mut!((*runtime_pointer).saved_rsp[0]),
214:                     new_rsp as *mut usize,
215:                     core::ptr::addr_of_mut!((*runtime_pointer).fx_state[0]),
216:                     core::ptr::addr_of_mut!((*runtime_pointer).fx_state[new_context]),
217:                 );
218:             }
219:         }
220:         restore_interrupts(initial_flags);
221: 
222:         loop {
223:             let flags = disable_interrupts();
224:             let (new_context, new_rsp, finished) = {
225:                 // SAFETY: No other thread runs while IF is clear. All mutable
226:                 // scheduler borrows end before any subsequent context switch.
227:                 let runtime = unsafe { &mut *runtime_pointer };
228:                 runtime.wake_ready_events();
229:                 if runtime.all_threads_exited() {
230:                     (None, None, true)
231:                 } else {
232:                     match runtime.policy.dispatch() {
233:                         Ok(Some(thread)) => {
234:                             let context = thread.slot_index() + 1;
235:                             runtime.current_context = context;
236:                             (Some(context), Some(runtime.saved_rsp[context]), false)
237:                         }
238:                         Ok(None) => (None, None, false),
239:                         Err(_) => {
240:                             THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
241:                             (None, None, true)
242:                         }
243:                     }
244:                 }
245:             };
246:             if finished {
247:                 ACTIVE_RUNTIME.store(core::ptr::null_mut(), Ordering::Release);
248:                 unsafe { save_bootstrap_fx_state() };
249:                 restore_interrupts(flags);
250:                 return;
251:             }
252:             if let (Some(new_context), Some(new_rsp)) = (new_context, new_rsp) {
253:                 // SAFETY: The bootstrap context is currently active and the
254:                 // selected stack and FXSAVE area remain owned by this runtime.
255:                 unsafe {
256:                     rustix_arch_context_switch(
257:                         core::ptr::addr_of_mut!((*runtime_pointer).saved_rsp[0]),
258:                         new_rsp as *mut usize,
259:                         core::ptr::addr_of_mut!((*runtime_pointer).fx_state[0]),
260:                         core::ptr::addr_of_mut!((*runtime_pointer).fx_state[new_context]),
261:                     );
262:                 }
263:                 restore_interrupts(flags);
264:             } else {
265:                 // `sti; hlt` is atomic with respect to the interrupt shadow, so
266:                 // a PIT wakeup cannot be lost between checking deadlines and halt.
267:                 // SAFETY: The verified PIT handler is installed and the only
268:                 // unmasked device route; it performs no allocation or scheduling.
269:                 unsafe { asm!("sti; hlt") };
270:             }
271:         }
272:     }
273: }
274: 
275: /// Run a single long-lived cooperative worker on an owned guarded stack.
276: pub fn run_shell<M: CandidatePageTableMemory>(
277:     candidate: &mut CandidatePageTables<'_, M>,
278:     allocator: *mut FrameAllocator,
279:     cpu_info: &crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::CpuInfo,
280:     ticks: &'static AtomicTickCounter,
281:     entry: extern "C" fn(*mut (), usize),
282:     argument: usize,
283: ) -> ! {
284:     let mut runtime =
285:         KernelThreadRuntime::new(ticks).unwrap_or_else(|_| halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure());
286:     runtime.input_events_enabled = true;
287:     runtime.enable_thread_interrupts = true;
288:     let runtime_pointer = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(runtime);
289:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
290:     // SAFETY: The caller supplies the live allocator retained by BootInfo. This
291:     // exclusive borrow is limited to stack setup and ends before context switches.
292:     let spawned = unsafe {
293:         spawn_thread(
294:             &mut runtime,
295:             candidate,
296:             &mut *allocator,
297:             cpu_info,
298:             runtime_pointer,
299:             entry,
300:             argument,
301:         )
302:     };
303:     if spawned.is_err() {
304:         restore_interrupts(flags);
305:         halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure();
306:     }
307:     restore_interrupts(flags);
308:     // SAFETY: The caller keeps the active root, allocator, CPU snapshot, thread
309:     // context, and callback argument alive for this non-returning kernel runtime.
310:     unsafe { KernelThreadRuntime::run_from_raw(runtime_pointer) };
311:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
312:     // SAFETY: No worker is running after `run_from_raw` returns, so the raw
313:     // allocator pointer is exclusively available for final stack cleanup.
314:     let allocator = unsafe { &mut *allocator };
315:     reap_exited_stacks(&mut runtime, candidate, allocator);
316:     restore_interrupts(flags);
317:     halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure()
318: }
319: 
320: /// Own scheduler state and the guarded stacks prepared for a bounded process run.
321: /// The value must stay at a stable address after stack preparation because each
322: /// synthetic thread context retains a pointer to its runtime.
323: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
324: pub(crate) struct PreparedProcessTasks {
325:     runtime: KernelThreadRuntime,
326:     count: usize,
327:     _pin: core::marker::PhantomPinned,
328: }
329: 
330: /// Construct an empty process-task set before preparing its stacks.
331: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
332: pub(crate) fn new_process_tasks(
333:     ticks: &'static AtomicTickCounter,
334: ) -> Result<PreparedProcessTasks, &'static str> {
335:     Ok(PreparedProcessTasks {
336:         runtime: KernelThreadRuntime::new(ticks)
337:             .map_err(|_| "process scheduler wait-queue capacity exhausted")?,
338:         count: 0,
339:         _pin: core::marker::PhantomPinned,
340:     })
341: }
342: 
343: /// Map scheduler-owned stacks before process roots borrow the active candidate's
344: /// kernel half. The prepared runtime must remain at the same address afterward.
345: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
346: pub(crate) fn prepare_process_task_stacks<M: CandidatePageTableMemory>(
347:     prepared: core::pin::Pin<&mut PreparedProcessTasks>,
348:     candidate: &mut CandidatePageTables<'_, M>,
349:     allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
350:     cpu_info: &crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::CpuInfo,
351:     count: usize,
352: ) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
353:     // SAFETY: The caller pins this !Unpin runtime before any thread stores its
354:     // address in a synthetic context; this function never moves the runtime.
355:     let prepared = unsafe { prepared.get_unchecked_mut() };
356:     if count == 0 || count > MAX_THREADS || prepared.count != 0 {
357:         return Err("process thread set is empty, exceeds capacity, or was already prepared");
358:     }
359:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
360:     let runtime_pointer = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(prepared.runtime);
361:     for _ in 0..count {
362:         // The placeholder is replaced before dispatch; creating the scheduler
363:         // context now maps its stack before process roots share the kernel half.
364:         let spawned = spawn_thread(
365:             &mut prepared.runtime,
366:             candidate,
367:             allocator,
368:             cpu_info,
369:             runtime_pointer,
370:             unstarted_process_task,
371:             0,
372:         );
373:         if spawned.is_err() {
374:             cancel_unstarted_threads(&mut prepared.runtime);
375:             reap_exited_stacks(&mut prepared.runtime, candidate, allocator);
376:             restore_interrupts(flags);
377:             return Err("process kernel stack preparation failed");
378:         }
379:         prepared.count += 1;
380:     }
381:     restore_interrupts(flags);
382:     Ok(())
383: }
384: 
385: /// Run prepared tasks with IF disabled; process waits are explicit scheduler
386: /// block operations, not timer-preempted user contexts.
387: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
388: pub(crate) fn run_prepared_processes(
389:     prepared: core::pin::Pin<&mut PreparedProcessTasks>,
390:     entries: &[(ThreadEntry, usize)],
391: ) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
392:     let runtime_pointer = {
393:         // SAFETY: The caller maintains the pin until every scheduler context exits.
394:         let prepared = unsafe { prepared.get_unchecked_mut() };
395:         if entries.len() != prepared.count || prepared.count == 0 {
396:             return Err("process task entries do not match prepared stack count");
397:         }
398:         for (slot, (entry, argument)) in entries.iter().copied().enumerate() {
399:             let Some(thread) = prepared.runtime.thread_ids[slot] else {
400:                 return Err("process task context is not ready for initial dispatch");
401:             };
402:             if prepared.runtime.policy.state(thread) != Ok(ThreadState::Ready) {
403:                 return Err("process task context is not ready for initial dispatch");
404:             }
405:             prepared.runtime.entries[slot] = Some(entry);
406:             prepared.runtime.arguments[slot] = argument;
407:         }
408:         core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(prepared.runtime)
409:     };
410:     // SAFETY: Runtime and callback contexts remain at stable addresses until all
411:     // tasks exit; no Rust mutable reference spans the scheduler context switches.
412:     unsafe { KernelThreadRuntime::run_from_raw(runtime_pointer) };
413:     // SAFETY: Every synthetic context has returned to the bootstrap stack; the
414:     // runtime remains pinned and exclusively owned by this caller.
415:     if !unsafe { &*runtime_pointer }.all_threads_exited() {
416:         halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure();
417:     }
418:     Ok(())
419: }
420: 
421: /// Reclaim the scheduler stacks after all roots that shared their kernel mappings
422: /// have been destroyed and their borrow of the candidate root has ended.
423: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
424: pub(crate) fn reclaim_process_task_stacks<M: CandidatePageTableMemory>(
425:     prepared: core::pin::Pin<&mut PreparedProcessTasks>,
426:     candidate: &mut CandidatePageTables<'_, M>,
427:     allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
428: ) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
429:     // SAFETY: The caller keeps the runtime pinned until all saved contexts are
430:     // gone and their stack mappings have been reclaimed.
431:     let prepared = unsafe { prepared.get_unchecked_mut() };
432:     if !prepared.runtime.all_threads_exited() {
433:         cancel_unstarted_threads(&mut prepared.runtime);
434:     }
435:     if !prepared.runtime.all_threads_exited() {
436:         halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure();
437:     }
438:     let frames_before = allocator.free_frames();
439:     let expected_frames = (prepared.count * THREAD_STACK_PAGES) as u64;
440:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
441:     reap_exited_stacks(&mut prepared.runtime, candidate, allocator);
442:     prepared.count = 0;
443:     restore_interrupts(flags);
444:     if allocator.free_frames().checked_sub(frames_before) != Some(expected_frames) {
445:         return Err("scheduler task stack frames were not fully reclaimed");
446:     }
447:     Ok(())
448: }
449: 
450: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
451: extern "C" fn unstarted_process_task(_runtime: *mut (), _argument: usize) {}
452: 
453: /// Block the current cooperative thread until an input IRQ publishes an event.
454: pub(crate) fn wait_for_input(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) {
455:     // SAFETY: Only a normal cooperative thread calls this; IRQ handlers merely
456:     // publish the atomic event and never touch scheduler policy state.
457:     if !unsafe { block_current(runtime_pointer.cast(), None) } {
458:         THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
459:     }
460: }
461: 
462: /// Block the current process task until a process-start event or runtime closure.
463: /// Returns `true` after any wake so the caller can recheck its published slot.
464: ///
465: /// # Safety
466: /// `runtime_pointer` must point to the live, pinned process-task runtime that
467: /// owns the current cooperative task. The call may context-switch; no other CPU
468: /// may access this single-vCPU runtime.
469: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
470: pub(crate) unsafe fn wait_for_process_start(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) -> bool {
471:     if runtime_pointer.is_null() {
472:         return false;
473:     }
474:     let runtime = runtime_pointer.cast::<KernelThreadRuntime>();
475:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
476:     // SAFETY: The caller keeps this runtime pinned and alive, while IF
477:     // exclusion serializes the closure read with queueing and wakeup.
478:     let (closed, queue) = unsafe {
479:         let runtime = &*runtime;
480:         (runtime.process_start_closed, runtime.process_start_queue)
481:     };
482:     if closed {
483:         restore_interrupts(flags);
484:         return false;
485:     }
486:     // IF remains disabled between checking closure and entering the wait queue,
487:     // so a close cannot be lost in that interval. Multiple starts share this
488:     // queue; each task checks its own published slot after it wakes.
489:     // SAFETY: The same pinned runtime owns the current cooperative task; the
490:     // helper releases its mutable scheduler borrow before switching.
491:     let blocked = unsafe { block_current_on_queue(runtime, queue) };
492:     restore_interrupts(flags);
493:     blocked
494: }
495: 
496: /// Publish a ready process context and wake deferred scheduler tasks.
497: ///
498: /// The caller must publish the process context and all callback state before
499: /// calling this function. Tasks that have not yet blocked observe their own
500: /// published slot before waiting.
501: ///
502: /// # Safety
503: /// `runtime_pointer` must point to the live, pinned process-task runtime. The
504: /// published process context must remain valid until its task exits, and the
505: /// caller must obey the runtime's single-vCPU ownership contract.
506: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
507: pub(crate) unsafe fn wake_process_start_waiter(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) -> bool {
508:     if runtime_pointer.is_null() {
509:         return false;
510:     }
511:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
512:     // SAFETY: IF is clear and the caller guarantees the pinned runtime remains
513:     // live; no mutable reference survives the scheduler operation.
514:     let result = unsafe {
515:         let runtime = &mut *runtime_pointer.cast::<KernelThreadRuntime>();
516:         if runtime.process_start_closed {
517:             false
518:         } else {
519:             runtime.policy.wake_all(runtime.process_start_queue).is_ok()
520:         }
521:     };
522:     restore_interrupts(flags);
523:     result
524: }
525: 
526: /// Close process start and release a task that never received a start context.
527: /// This is idempotent and also wakes a task already blocked on the start queue.
528: ///
529: /// # Safety
530: /// `runtime_pointer` must point to the live, pinned process-task runtime, and
531: /// the caller must obey the runtime's single-vCPU ownership contract.
532: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
533: pub(crate) unsafe fn close_process_start(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) -> bool {
534:     if runtime_pointer.is_null() {
535:         return false;
536:     }
537:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
538:     // SAFETY: IF is clear and the runtime remains live through process execution.
539:     let result = unsafe {
540:         let runtime = &mut *runtime_pointer.cast::<KernelThreadRuntime>();
541:         runtime.process_start_closed = true;
542:         runtime.policy.wake_all(runtime.process_start_queue).is_ok()
543:     };
544:     restore_interrupts(flags);
545:     result
546: }
547: 
548: /// Block the current process thread until some process publishes an exit.
549: ///
550: /// # Safety
551: /// `runtime_pointer` must point to the live, pinned process-task runtime that
552: /// owns the currently running cooperative thread. The call may context-switch.
553: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
554: pub(crate) unsafe fn wait_for_process_exit(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) -> bool {
555:     if runtime_pointer.is_null() {
556:         return false;
557:     }
558:     // SAFETY: The pointer is installed by the prepared process-task runtime and remains live until
559:     // all process tasks exit; this call is made only from a cooperative thread.
560:     let runtime = runtime_pointer.cast::<KernelThreadRuntime>();
561:     let queue = unsafe { core::ptr::addr_of!((*runtime).process_exit_queue).read() };
562:     // SAFETY: The runtime and queue remain live, and the scheduler owns this
563:     // single-vCPU policy while local interrupts are disabled by the helper.
564:     unsafe { block_current_on_queue(runtime_pointer.cast(), queue) }
565: }
566: 
567: /// Wake all process waiters after a process exit has been recorded.
568: ///
569: /// # Safety
570: /// `runtime_pointer` must point to the live, pinned process-task runtime, and
571: /// the caller must hold the single-vCPU scheduler's IF-disabled execution state.
572: #[cfg(feature = "probe-user-process")]
573: pub(crate) unsafe fn wake_process_exit_waiters(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) -> bool {
574:     if runtime_pointer.is_null() {
575:         return false;
576:     }
577:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
578:     // SAFETY: IF is clear and the runtime remains live throughout process execution.
579:     let result = unsafe {
580:         let runtime = &mut *runtime_pointer.cast::<KernelThreadRuntime>();
581:         runtime.policy.wake_all(runtime.process_exit_queue).is_ok()
582:     };
583:     restore_interrupts(flags);
584:     result
585: }
586: 
587: /// Exercise real switching, stack guards, event wakeup, timed sleep, and timer preemption.
588: /// The caller must provide the current active root, keep this single-vCPU kernel
589: /// path alive for the call, and have already enabled the verified PIT route.
590: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
591: pub fn smoke<M: CandidatePageTableMemory>(
592:     candidate: &mut CandidatePageTables<'_, M>,
593:     allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
594:     cpu_info: &crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::CpuInfo,
595:     ticks: &'static AtomicTickCounter,
596: ) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
597:     THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(0, Ordering::Release);
598:     if !irq_spin_lock_preserves_interrupt_state() {
599:         return Err("IRQ-save spin lock failed to mask or restore IF");
600:     }
601:     kprintln!("IRQ-save lock path passed: prior IF state preserved");
602: 
603:     let mut runtime = KernelThreadRuntime::new(ticks).map_err(|_| "scheduler setup failed")?;
604:     runtime.enable_thread_interrupts = true;
605:     let runtime_pointer = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(runtime);
606: 
607:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
608:     let first = spawn_thread(
609:         &mut runtime,
610:         candidate,
611:         allocator,
612:         cpu_info,
613:         runtime_pointer,
614:         waiter_thread,
615:         0,
616:     );
617:     let second = if first.is_ok() {
618:         spawn_thread(
619:             &mut runtime,
620:             candidate,
621:             allocator,
622:             cpu_info,
623:             runtime_pointer,
624:             signaler_thread,
625:             0,
626:         )
627:     } else {
628:         Err("waiter thread stack setup failed")
629:     };
630:     restore_interrupts(flags);
631: 
632:     if first.is_err() || second.is_err() {
633:         cancel_unstarted_threads(&mut runtime);
634:         let flags = disable_interrupts();
635:         reap_exited_stacks(&mut runtime, candidate, allocator);
636:         restore_interrupts(flags);
637:         return Err(first
638:             .err()
639:             .or(second.err())
640:             .unwrap_or("thread setup failed"));
641:     }
642: 
643:     // Every stack slot was checked page by page during setup: the lower guard is
644:     // absent, and all four stack leaves are supervisor RW/NX with the selected WB type.
645:     kprintln!("kernel thread stacks verified: mapped RW/NX with absent lower guards");
646:     // SAFETY: The runtime remains on the bootstrap stack and is passed by raw
647:     // pointer; no mutable reference is retained across the context switches.
648:     unsafe { KernelThreadRuntime::run_from_raw(runtime_pointer) };
649: 
650:     let passed = THREAD_TEST_TRACE.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 5
651:         && ticks.ticks() >= 2
652:         && runtime.all_threads_exited();
653:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
654:     reap_exited_stacks(&mut runtime, candidate, allocator);
655:     restore_interrupts(flags);
656:     if !passed {
657:         return Err("cooperative yield/wakeup/timed-sleep sequence failed");
658:     }
659:     kprintln!("cooperative thread path passed: yield, event wakeup, timed sleep, stack guards");
660:     timer_preemption_smoke(candidate, allocator, cpu_info, ticks)
661: }
662: 
663: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
664: fn irq_spin_lock_preserves_interrupt_state() -> bool {
665:     use rustix_kernel::sync::IrqSpinLock;
666: 
667:     fn interrupts_enabled() -> bool {
668:         let flags: u64;
669:         // SAFETY: Reading RFLAGS is non-mutating in the CPL 0 QEMU probe.
670:         unsafe { asm!("pushfq", "pop {}", out(reg) flags) };
671:         flags & (1 << 9) != 0
672:     }
673: 
674:     let lock = IrqSpinLock::new(());
675:     let initially_enabled = interrupts_enabled();
676:     let masked_while_held = {
677:         let _guard = lock.lock();
678:         !interrupts_enabled()
679:     };
680:     let restored_after_enabled = interrupts_enabled() == initially_enabled;
681: 
682:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
683:     let masked_while_already_disabled = {
684:         let _guard = lock.lock();
685:         !interrupts_enabled()
686:     };
687:     let remained_disabled_after_drop = !interrupts_enabled();
688:     restore_interrupts(flags);
689: 
690:     initially_enabled
691:         && masked_while_held
692:         && restored_after_enabled
693:         && masked_while_already_disabled
694:         && remained_disabled_after_drop
695: }
696: 
697: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
698: fn timer_preemption_smoke<M: CandidatePageTableMemory>(
699:     candidate: &mut CandidatePageTables<'_, M>,
700:     allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
701:     cpu_info: &crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::CpuInfo,
702:     ticks: &'static AtomicTickCounter,
703: ) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
704:     PREEMPTION_A_STARTED.store(0, Ordering::Release);
705:     PREEMPTION_B_STARTED.store(0, Ordering::Release);
706:     PREEMPTION_TEST_FAILED.store(0, Ordering::Release);
707:     PREEMPTION_FPU_FAILED.store(0, Ordering::Release);
708: 
709:     let mut runtime =
710:         KernelThreadRuntime::new(ticks).map_err(|_| "preemption scheduler setup failed")?;
711:     runtime.enable_thread_interrupts = true;
712:     let runtime_pointer = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(runtime);
713:     let ticks_pointer = ticks as *const AtomicTickCounter as usize;
714: 
715:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
716:     let first = spawn_thread(
717:         &mut runtime,
718:         candidate,
719:         allocator,
720:         cpu_info,
721:         runtime_pointer,
722:         non_yielding_preemption_worker_a,
723:         ticks_pointer,
724:     );
725:     let second = if first.is_ok() {
726:         spawn_thread(
727:             &mut runtime,
728:             candidate,
729:             allocator,
730:             cpu_info,
731:             runtime_pointer,
732:             non_yielding_preemption_worker_b,
733:             ticks_pointer,
734:         )
735:     } else {
736:         Err("first preemption worker stack setup failed")
737:     };
738:     restore_interrupts(flags);
739: 
740:     if first.is_err() || second.is_err() {
741:         cancel_unstarted_threads(&mut runtime);
742:         let flags = disable_interrupts();
743:         reap_exited_stacks(&mut runtime, candidate, allocator);
744:         restore_interrupts(flags);
745:         return Err(first
746:             .err()
747:             .or(second.err())
748:             .unwrap_or("preemption worker setup failed"));
749:     }
750: 
751:     // Both workers busy-wait without yielding. Worker B can start only after a
752:     // PIT interrupt preempts worker A. Worker A also checks that its live XMM0
753:     // value survives the interrupt handler and another thread's execution.
754:     unsafe { KernelThreadRuntime::run_from_raw(runtime_pointer) };
755:     let passed = PREEMPTION_A_STARTED.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 1
756:         && PREEMPTION_B_STARTED.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 1
757:         && PREEMPTION_TEST_FAILED.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 0
758:         && PREEMPTION_FPU_FAILED.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 0
759:         && runtime.all_threads_exited();
760:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
761:     reap_exited_stacks(&mut runtime, candidate, allocator);
762:     restore_interrupts(flags);
763:     if !passed {
764:         return Err("timer did not preempt a non-yielding thread within the tick budget");
765:     }
766:     kprintln!("timer preemption path passed: non-yielding workers and FPU state survived");
767:     Ok(())
768: }
769: 
770: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
771: extern "C" fn non_yielding_preemption_worker_a(_runtime: *mut (), ticks_pointer: usize) {
772:     PREEMPTION_A_STARTED.store(1, Ordering::Release);
773:     let ticks = unsafe { &*(ticks_pointer as *const AtomicTickCounter) };
774:     let deadline = ticks.ticks().saturating_add(PREEMPTION_TEST_TICK_BUDGET);
775:     unsafe {
776:         asm!(
777:             "movdqu xmm0, xmmword ptr [{pattern}]",
778:             pattern = in(reg) PREEMPTION_PATTERN_A.as_ptr(),
779:             out("xmm0") _,
780:             options(readonly, nostack, preserves_flags),
781:         );
782:     }
783:     while PREEMPTION_B_STARTED.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 0 {
784:         if ticks.ticks() >= deadline {
785:             PREEMPTION_TEST_FAILED.store(1, Ordering::Release);
786:             break;
787:         }
788:         spin_loop();
789:     }
790:     let mut saved_state = core::mem::MaybeUninit::<FxSaveArea>::uninit();
791:     // SAFETY: `FxSaveArea` is 16-byte aligned and exactly 512 bytes; FXSAVE
792:     // writes the complete legacy x87/SSE state image before it is inspected.
793:     unsafe {
794:         asm!(
795:             "fxsave64 [{state}]",
796:             state = in(reg) saved_state.as_mut_ptr(),
797:             options(nostack),
798:         );
799:     }
800:     // SAFETY: The preceding FXSAVE initialized every byte of the state image.
801:     let saved_state = unsafe { saved_state.assume_init() };
802:     if saved_state.0[160..176] != PREEMPTION_PATTERN_A {
803:         PREEMPTION_FPU_FAILED.store(1, Ordering::Release);
804:     }
805: }
806: 
807: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
808: extern "C" fn non_yielding_preemption_worker_b(_runtime: *mut (), _ticks_pointer: usize) {
809:     unsafe {
810:         asm!(
811:             "movdqu xmm0, xmmword ptr [{pattern}]",
812:             pattern = in(reg) PREEMPTION_PATTERN_B.as_ptr(),
813:             out("xmm0") _,
814:             options(readonly, nostack, preserves_flags),
815:         );
816:     }
817:     PREEMPTION_B_STARTED.store(1, Ordering::Release);
818: }
819: 
820: fn spawn_thread<M: CandidatePageTableMemory>(
821:     runtime: &mut KernelThreadRuntime,
822:     candidate: &mut CandidatePageTables<'_, M>,
823:     allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
824:     cpu_info: &crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::CpuInfo,
825:     runtime_pointer: *mut KernelThreadRuntime,
826:     entry: ThreadEntry,
827:     argument: usize,
828: ) -> Result<ThreadId, &'static str> {
829:     let slot = runtime
830:         .thread_ids
831:         .iter()
832:         .position(Option::is_none)
833:         .ok_or("thread capacity exhausted")?;
834:     let region_start = THREAD_STACK_VIRTUAL_BASE
835:         .checked_add(
836:             (slot as u64)
837:                 .checked_mul(THREAD_STACK_STRIDE)
838:                 .ok_or("stack VA overflow")?,
839:         )
840:         .ok_or("stack VA overflow")?;
841:     let stack_start = region_start
842:         .checked_add(PAGE_SIZE)
843:         .ok_or("stack VA overflow")?;
844: 
845:     for page in 0..=THREAD_STACK_PAGES {
846:         let address = region_start + page as u64 * PAGE_SIZE;
847:         if !matches!(
848:             candidate.walk(address),
849:             Ok(PageWalkOutcome::NotPresent { .. })
850:         ) {
851:             return Err("thread guard or stack VA is already mapped");
852:         }
853:     }
854: 
855:     let physical_width = cpu_info
856:         .physical_address_bits
857:         .ok_or("physical width unavailable")?;
858:     let pat = PatLayout::from_msr_value(cpu_info.pat_msr_value.ok_or("PAT unavailable")?)
859:         .map_err(|_| "PAT state invalid")?;
860:     let mtrrs = match &cpu_info.mtrr {
861:         MtrrState::Captured(registers) => registers
862:             .validated_layout(physical_width)
863:             .map_err(|_| "MTRR state invalid")?,
864:         _ => return Err("MTRR state unavailable"),
865:     };
866:     let permissions = PagePermissions {
867:         writable: true,
868:         executable: false,
869:         user_accessible: false,
870:     };
871:     let mut mapped_pages = 0usize;
872:     for page in 0..THREAD_STACK_PAGES {
873:         let virtual_address = stack_start + page as u64 * PAGE_SIZE;
874:         let frame = allocator.allocate().ok_or_else(|| {
875:             rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, mapped_pages);
876:             "physical frames exhausted for thread stack"
877:         })?;
878:         let mapping = match validate_mapping_range(
879:             virtual_address,
880:             frame.address(),
881:             PAGE_SIZE,
882:             permissions,
883:             Some(physical_width),
884:             cpu_info.active_linear_address_bits,
885:         ) {
886:             Ok(mapping) => mapping,
887:             Err(_) => {
888:                 deallocate_or_halt(allocator, frame);
889:                 rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, mapped_pages);
890:                 return Err("thread stack mapping rejected");
891:             }
892:         };
893:         let attributes = match mapping.select_cache_attributes(
894:             CachePolicy::WriteBack,
895:             pat,
896:             &mtrrs,
897:             cpu_info.normal_caching,
898:         ) {
899:             Ok(attributes) if attributes.effective_type() == EffectiveMemoryType::WriteBack => {
900:                 attributes
901:             }
902:             _ => {
903:                 deallocate_or_halt(allocator, frame);
904:                 rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, mapped_pages);
905:                 return Err("thread stack has no uniform write-back policy");
906:             }
907:         };
908:         // SAFETY: The active root is current on this single CPU; IF is clear
909:         // throughout stack setup, and each mapping uses an allocator-owned frame.
910:         if let Err(failure) =
911:             unsafe { candidate.map_owned_page_active(allocator, frame, mapping, attributes) }
912:         {
913:             deallocate_or_halt(allocator, failure.frame);
914:             rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, mapped_pages);
915:             return Err("thread stack mapping failed");
916:         }
917:         match candidate.walk(virtual_address) {
918:             Ok(PageWalkOutcome::Mapped(translation))
919:                 if translation.permissions == permissions
920:                     && translation.pat_index == attributes.pat_index() => {}
921:             _ => {
922:                 rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, mapped_pages + 1);
923:                 return Err("thread stack page-table verification failed");
924:             }
925:         }
926:         mapped_pages += 1;
927:     }
928: 
929:     let stack_top = stack_start + THREAD_STACK_PAGES as u64 * PAGE_SIZE;
930:     let frame_word_count = 16;
931:     let frame_words = unsafe {
932:         core::slice::from_raw_parts_mut(
933:             (stack_top as *mut usize).sub(frame_word_count),
934:             frame_word_count,
935:         )
936:     };
937:     let registers = [0, slot, runtime_pointer as usize, 0, 0, 0];
938:     let saved_rsp = initialize_thread_context_stack(
939:         frame_words,
940:         registers,
941:         kernel_thread_trampoline as *const () as usize,
942:     );
943:     let Some(saved_rsp) = saved_rsp else {
944:         rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, mapped_pages);
945:         return Err("thread initial context frame did not fit");
946:     };
947:     let thread = match runtime.policy.create_thread() {
948:         Ok(thread) if thread.slot_index() == slot => thread,
949:         Ok(_) => {
950:             rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, mapped_pages);
951:             return Err("scheduler slot and stack slot diverged");
952:         }
953:         Err(_) => {
954:             rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, mapped_pages);
955:             return Err("scheduler thread capacity exhausted");
956:         }
957:     };
958:     runtime.thread_ids[slot] = Some(thread);
959:     runtime.stack_virtual_starts[slot] = Some(stack_start);
960:     runtime.entries[slot] = Some(entry);
961:     runtime.arguments[slot] = argument;
962:     runtime.saved_rsp[slot + 1] = saved_rsp;
963:     Ok(thread)
964: }
965: 
966: fn rollback_stack<M: CandidatePageTableMemory>(
967:     candidate: &mut CandidatePageTables<'_, M>,
968:     allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
969:     stack_start: u64,
970:     mapped_pages: usize,
971: ) {
972:     for page in (0..mapped_pages).rev() {
973:         let virtual_address = stack_start + page as u64 * PAGE_SIZE;
974:         // SAFETY: Stack setup keeps IF clear and this candidate is the active
975:         // root; the frame is not returned until `invlpg` completes.
976:         let frame = unsafe { candidate.unmap_owned_page_active(virtual_address) }
977:             .unwrap_or_else(|_| halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure());
978:         deallocate_or_halt(allocator, frame);
979:     }
980: }
981: 
982: fn reap_exited_stacks<M: CandidatePageTableMemory>(
983:     runtime: &mut KernelThreadRuntime,
984:     candidate: &mut CandidatePageTables<'_, M>,
985:     allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
986: ) {
987:     for slot in 0..MAX_THREADS {
988:         let Some(thread) = runtime.thread_ids[slot] else {
989:             continue;
990:         };
991:         if runtime.policy.state(thread) != Ok(ThreadState::Exited) {
992:             continue;
993:         }
994:         let stack_start =
995:             runtime.stack_virtual_starts[slot].unwrap_or_else(|| halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure());
996:         rollback_stack(candidate, allocator, stack_start, THREAD_STACK_PAGES);
997:         runtime
998:             .policy
999:             .reap(thread)
1000:             .unwrap_or_else(|_| halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure());
1001:         runtime.thread_ids[slot] = None;
1002:         runtime.stack_virtual_starts[slot] = None;
1003:         runtime.entries[slot] = None;
1004:         runtime.arguments[slot] = 0;
1005:         runtime.deadlines[slot] = None;
1006:     }
1007: }
1008: 
1009: #[cfg(any(feature = "probe-user-process", feature = "probe-kernel-threads"))]
1010: fn cancel_unstarted_threads(runtime: &mut KernelThreadRuntime) {
1011:     loop {
1012:         match runtime.policy.dispatch() {
1013:             Ok(Some(_)) => {
1014:                 if runtime.policy.exit_current().is_err() {
1015:                     halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure();
1016:                 }
1017:             }
1018:             Ok(None) => return,
1019:             Err(_) => halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure(),
1020:         }
1021:     }
1022: }
1023: 
1024: fn deallocate_or_halt(allocator: &mut FrameAllocator, frame: Frame) {
1025:     if let Err(failure) = allocator.deallocate(frame) {
1026:         kprintln!("thread stack frame release failed: {:?}", failure.error);
1027:         halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure();
1028:     }
1029: }
1030: 
1031: fn halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure() -> ! {
1032:     loop {
1033:         // SAFETY: Losing stack-frame ownership is fatal; stop without touching
1034:         // any potentially inconsistent scheduler or allocator state.
1035:         unsafe { asm!("cli; hlt", options(nomem, nostack)) };
1036:     }
1037: }
1038: 
1039: extern "C" fn kernel_thread_trampoline() -> ! {
1040:     let runtime_pointer: *mut KernelThreadRuntime;
1041:     let slot: usize;
1042:     // SAFETY: The initial stack frame restores the runtime pointer in R13 and
1043:     // the generation-checked policy slot index in R14 before this entry.
1044:     unsafe {
1045:         asm!("mov {}, r13", out(reg) runtime_pointer, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags));
1046:         asm!("mov {}, r14", out(reg) slot, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags));
1047:     }
1048:     let (entry, argument) = {
1049:         // SAFETY: The runtime is live on the retained bootstrap stack and the
1050:         // selected slot was initialized before its context became dispatchable.
1051:         let runtime = unsafe { &*runtime_pointer };
1052:         let entry = runtime.entries[slot].unwrap_or_else(|| halt_on_stack_cleanup_failure());
1053:         (entry, runtime.arguments[slot])
1054:     };
1055:     if unsafe { (*runtime_pointer).enable_thread_interrupts } {
1056:         // Kernel shell contexts enable the already-verified timer route only after
1057:         // scheduler state is ready. User-process contexts deliberately keep IF clear.
1058:         // SAFETY: The shell caller configures the timer and registered handler first.
1059:         unsafe { asm!("sti") };
1060:     }
1061:     entry(runtime_pointer.cast(), argument);
1062:     // SAFETY: This thread's entry returned normally; mark it exited and switch
1063:     // away before its stack can be reclaimed by the bootstrap context.
1064:     unsafe { exit_current(runtime_pointer) }
1065: }
1066: 
1067: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
1068: extern "C" fn waiter_thread(runtime: *mut (), _argument: usize) {
1069:     THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(1, Ordering::Release);
1070:     if !wait_for_event(runtime) || THREAD_TEST_TRACE.load(Ordering::Acquire) != 2 {
1071:         THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
1072:         return;
1073:     }
1074:     THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(3, Ordering::Release);
1075:     if !sleep_for_ticks(runtime, 2) || THREAD_TEST_TRACE.load(Ordering::Acquire) != 4 {
1076:         THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
1077:         return;
1078:     }
1079:     THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(5, Ordering::Release);
1080: }
1081: 
1082: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
1083: extern "C" fn signaler_thread(runtime: *mut (), _argument: usize) {
1084:     if THREAD_TEST_TRACE.load(Ordering::Acquire) != 1 {
1085:         THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
1086:         return;
1087:     }
1088:     THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(2, Ordering::Release);
1089:     if !wake_event(runtime) {
1090:         THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
1091:         return;
1092:     }
1093:     yield_current(runtime);
1094:     if THREAD_TEST_TRACE.load(Ordering::Acquire) != 3 {
1095:         THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
1096:         return;
1097:     }
1098:     THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(4, Ordering::Release);
1099:     while THREAD_TEST_TRACE.load(Ordering::Acquire) != 5 {
1100:         yield_current(runtime);
1101:     }
1102: }
1103: 
1104: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
1105: fn wait_for_event(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) -> bool {
1106:     // SAFETY: This is an ordinary cooperative thread, never an IRQ handler.
1107:     unsafe { block_current(runtime_pointer.cast(), None) }
1108: }
1109: 
1110: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
1111: fn sleep_for_ticks(runtime_pointer: *mut (), delay: u64) -> bool {
1112:     if delay == 0 {
1113:         return true;
1114:     }
1115:     // SAFETY: The runtime pointer is restored from this thread's initial context;
1116:     // the runtime remains alive until every thread has exited and been reaped.
1117:     unsafe { block_current(runtime_pointer.cast(), Some(delay)) }
1118: }
1119: 
1120: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
1121: fn wake_event(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) -> bool {
1122:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
1123:     // SAFETY: IF is clear and only the current cooperative thread mutates policy.
1124:     let result = unsafe {
1125:         let runtime = &mut *runtime_pointer.cast::<KernelThreadRuntime>();
1126:         runtime.policy.wake_one(runtime.event_queue).is_ok()
1127:     };
1128:     restore_interrupts(flags);
1129:     result
1130: }
1131: 
1132: #[cfg(feature = "probe-kernel-threads")]
1133: fn yield_current(runtime_pointer: *mut ()) {
1134:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
1135:     let switch = unsafe {
1136:         let runtime = &mut *runtime_pointer.cast::<KernelThreadRuntime>();
1137:         runtime.wake_ready_events();
1138:         let Some(current) = runtime.policy.current() else {
1139:             restore_interrupts(flags);
1140:             return;
1141:         };
1142:         let old_context = runtime.current_context;
1143:         let next = match runtime.policy.yield_current() {
1144:             Ok(next) => next,
1145:             Err(_) => {
1146:                 THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
1147:                 restore_interrupts(flags);
1148:                 return;
1149:             }
1150:         };
1151:         let next_context = next.slot_index() + 1;
1152:         runtime.current_context = next_context;
1153:         if current == next {
1154:             None
1155:         } else {
1156:             Some((
1157:                 core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(runtime.saved_rsp[old_context]),
1158:                 runtime.saved_rsp[next_context],
1159:                 core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(runtime.fx_state[old_context]),
1160:                 core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(runtime.fx_state[next_context]),
1161:             ))
1162:         }
1163:     };
1164:     if let Some((old_rsp, new_rsp, old_fx_state, new_fx_state)) = switch {
1165:         // SAFETY: The selected contexts are same-CPU kernel stacks, and the
1166:         // temporary scheduler borrows end before switching their saved FPU state.
1167:         unsafe {
1168:             rustix_arch_context_switch(old_rsp, new_rsp as *mut usize, old_fx_state, new_fx_state)
1169:         };
1170:     }
1171:     restore_interrupts(flags);
1172: }
1173: 
1174: unsafe fn block_current(runtime_pointer: *mut KernelThreadRuntime, delay: Option<u64>) -> bool {
1175:     let flags = disable_interrupts();
1176:     let switch = {
1177:         // SAFETY: Called from one cooperative thread with IF clear; the state
1178:         // references are scoped entirely before the context switch.
1179:         let runtime = unsafe { &mut *runtime_pointer };
1180:         let input_event = runtime.wake_ready_events();

1302: fn disable_interrupts() -> u64 {
1303:     let flags: u64;
1304:     // SAFETY: Bootstrap and cooperative threads execute at CPL 0; RFLAGS is
1305:     // saved before CLI and the stack operation is balanced.
1306:     unsafe { asm!("pushfq", "pop {}", "cli", out(reg) flags) };
1307:     flags
1308: }
1309: 
1310: fn restore_interrupts(flags: u64) {
1311:     if flags & (1 << 9) != 0 {
1312:         // SAFETY: The caller only restores the prior IF state after its critical
1313:         // section and after the selected stack/context is fully established.
1314:         unsafe { asm!("sti") };
1315:     }
1316: }

1320:     new_rsp: *mut usize,

1322:     new_fx_state: *mut FxSaveArea,

1325:     // areas. Callers hold IF clear; publish the new area before it can receive IRQs.
1326:     unsafe { publish_fx_state(new_fx_state) };

1328:     // target context's initialized/saved FXSAVE image.
1329:     unsafe {
1330:         crate::arch::x86_64::context::context_switch(
1331:             old_rsp,

1333:             old_fx_state.cast(),
1334:             new_fx_state.cast(),
1335:         )
1336:     };
1337: }

1345:     // entering Rust. Update the return-path target before switching away.
1346:     unsafe { publish_fx_state(new_fx_state) };
1347:     // SAFETY: This is called only from the configured timer IRQ handler with IF
1348:     // clear; the target area's state is initialized and 16-byte aligned.
1349:     unsafe {
1350:         crate::arch::x86_64::context::context_switch_from_irq(old_rsp, new_rsp, new_fx_state.cast())
1351:     };

1354: unsafe fn publish_fx_state(state: *mut FxSaveArea) {
1355:     // Release pairs with the IRQ stub's plain load; the store is a single
1356:     // aligned 8-byte atomic, so the stub never observes a torn pointer.
1357:     ACTIVE_FX_STATE_POINTER.store(state, Ordering::Release);

1363:     // caller has IF clear while the last kernel thread returns to the bootstrap.
1364:     unsafe { asm!("fxsave64 [{}]", in(reg) state, options(nostack)) };
1365:     // SAFETY: Interrupts remain disabled until the saved bootstrap area is active.
1366:     unsafe { publish_fx_state(state) };

1371: pub(crate) fn timer_interrupt() {

1373:     if runtime_pointer.is_null() {
1374:         return;
1375:     }
1376: 
1377:     // The interrupt gate has cleared IF. Every ordinary scheduler mutation also

1380:     let switch = unsafe {
1381:         let runtime = &mut *runtime_pointer;

1384:             return;
1385:         };

1395:         runtime.current_context = next_context;

1398:         } else {

1400:                 core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(runtime.saved_rsp[old_context]),

1: //! Fixed-capacity policy state for single-vCPU kernel threads.
2: //!
3: //! This module does not own stacks, switch CPU contexts, or access hardware.
4: //! Its caller must serialize every call, including against timer IRQs (the
5: //! runtime does so by disabling local interrupts around policy mutations). A
6: //! thread is either ready, the sole running thread, blocked on exactly
7: //! one live wait queue, or exited. Exited slots remain occupied until `reap`.
8: //! Dispatch scans slots in deterministic round-robin order; waiters are woken
9: //! in FIFO order. All storage is inline and bounded by the const capacities.
10: 
11: /// Opaque identity for a thread slot allocation.
12: #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
13: pub struct ThreadId {
14:     slot: usize,
15:     generation: u64,
16: }
17: 
18: impl ThreadId {
19:     pub const fn slot_index(self) -> usize {
20:         self.slot
21:     }
22: }
23: 
24: /// Build the fresh stack frame consumed by the x86_64 cooperative switch.
25: ///
26: /// The saved-register order is R15, R14, R13, R12, RBP, RBX, followed by the
27: /// trampoline return address. The returned address is 16-byte aligned; after
28: /// six pops and `ret`, the trampoline observes the SysV entry alignment
29: /// (`RSP % 16 == 8`). This helper only prepares bounded inline stack storage.
30: pub fn initialize_thread_context_stack(
31:     stack: &mut [usize],
32:     saved_registers: [usize; 6],
33:     trampoline: usize,
34: ) -> Option<usize> {
35:     const REGISTER_SLOTS: usize = 6;
36:     const FRAME_SLOTS: usize = REGISTER_SLOTS + 1;
37:     let word_size = core::mem::size_of::<usize>();
38:     let frame_bytes = FRAME_SLOTS.checked_mul(word_size)?;
39:     let stack_bytes = stack.len().checked_mul(word_size)?;
40:     let stack_start = stack.as_mut_ptr() as usize;
41:     let stack_end = stack_start.checked_add(stack_bytes)?;
42:     let frame_start = stack_end.checked_sub(frame_bytes)? & !15usize;
43:     let frame_offset = frame_start.checked_sub(stack_start)?;
44:     if frame_offset % word_size != 0 {
45:         return None;
46:     }
47:     let first_slot = frame_offset / word_size;
48:     let frame_end = first_slot.checked_add(FRAME_SLOTS)?;
49:     if frame_end > stack.len() || frame_start & 15 != 0 {
50:         return None;
51:     }
52: 
53:     stack[first_slot..first_slot + REGISTER_SLOTS].copy_from_slice(&saved_registers);
54:     stack[first_slot + REGISTER_SLOTS] = trampoline;
55:     Some(frame_start)
56: }

58: /// Opaque identity for a wait-queue allocation.
59: #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
60: pub struct WaitQueueId {
61:     slot: usize,
62:     generation: u64,
63: }
64: 
65: /// Lifecycle state of a live thread slot.
66: #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
67: pub enum ThreadState {
68:     Ready,
69:     Running,
70:     Blocked(WaitQueueId),
71:     Exited,
72: }
73: 
74: /// Failures are explicit; rejected operations leave policy state unchanged.
75: #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
76: pub enum SchedulerError {
77:     ThreadCapacityFull,
78:     WaitQueueCapacityFull,
79:     WaiterCapacityFull,
80:     InvalidThread,
81:     InvalidWaitQueue,
82:     NoCurrentThread,
83:     CurrentThreadRunning,
84:     ThreadNotExited,
85:     WaitQueueNotEmpty,
86:     GenerationExhausted,
87:     InvalidTransition,
88: }
89: 
90: #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
91: struct ThreadSlot {
92:     generation: u64,
93:     state: Option<ThreadState>,
94: }
95: 
96: impl ThreadSlot {
97:     const EMPTY: Self = Self {
98:         generation: 0,
99:         state: None,
100:     };
101: }

104: struct WaitQueue<const WAITERS: usize> {
105:     generation: u64,
106:     active: bool,
107:     waiters: [Option<ThreadId>; WAITERS],

109: }
110:

120: /// Bounded scheduler policy with no allocation or architecture dependencies.
121: ///
122: /// `THREADS` bounds all live threads, including exited threads not yet reaped;
123: /// `QUEUES` bounds live wait queues; and `WAITERS` bounds each queue. The
124: /// scheduler enforces at most one running thread and consistent blocked/queue
125: /// membership, but its single-owner/single-CPU contract is caller-enforced.
126: pub struct Scheduler<const THREADS: usize, const QUEUES: usize, const WAITERS: usize> {
127:     threads: [ThreadSlot; THREADS],
128:     queues: [WaitQueue<WAITERS>; QUEUES],
129:     current: Option<ThreadId>,
130:     next_ready: usize,
131: }
132: 
133: impl<const THREADS: usize, const QUEUES: usize, const WAITERS: usize>
134:     Scheduler<THREADS, QUEUES, WAITERS>
135: {
136:     /// Create an empty scheduler. Zero capacities are permitted and fail
137:     /// explicitly when the corresponding resource is requested.
138:     pub const fn new() -> Self {
139:         Self {
140:             threads: [ThreadSlot::EMPTY; THREADS],
141:             queues: [WaitQueue::EMPTY; QUEUES],
142:             current: None,
143:             next_ready: 0,
144:         }
145:     }
146: 
147:     /// Add a ready thread, returning its generation-checked slot identity.
148:     pub fn create_thread(&mut self) -> Result<ThreadId, SchedulerError> {
149:         let mut generation_exhausted = false;
150:         for (index, slot) in self.threads.iter_mut().enumerate() {
151:             if slot.state.is_some() {
152:                 continue;
153:             }
154:             let Some(generation) = slot.generation.checked_add(1) else {
155:                 generation_exhausted = true;
156:                 continue;
157:             };
158:             slot.generation = generation;
159:             slot.state = Some(ThreadState::Ready);
160:             return Ok(ThreadId {
161:                 slot: index,
162:                 generation,
163:             });
164:         }
165:         if generation_exhausted {
166:             Err(SchedulerError::GenerationExhausted)
167:         } else {
168:             Err(SchedulerError::ThreadCapacityFull)
169:         }
170:     }
171: 
172:     /// Current running thread, if any.
173:     pub const fn current(&self) -> Option<ThreadId> {
174:         self.current
175:     }
176: 
177:     /// Query a live thread's lifecycle state. Stale and unknown IDs are rejected.
178:     pub fn state(&self, thread: ThreadId) -> Result<ThreadState, SchedulerError> {
179:         let slot = self.thread_slot(thread)?;
180:         slot.state.ok_or(SchedulerError::InvalidThread)
181:     }
182: 
183:     /// Select the next ready thread when the CPU has no running thread.
184:     pub fn dispatch(&mut self) -> Result<Option<ThreadId>, SchedulerError> {
185:         if self.current.is_some() {
186:             return Err(SchedulerError::CurrentThreadRunning);
187:         }
188:         Ok(self.select_ready())
189:     }
190: 
191:     /// Cooperatively yield the running thread and dispatch the next ready one.
192:     pub fn yield_current(&mut self) -> Result<ThreadId, SchedulerError> {
193:         self.preempt_current()
194:     }
195: 
196:     /// Put the running thread at the end of the round-robin turn and select the
197:     /// next ready thread. Timer-driven preemption uses the same policy transition.
198:     pub fn preempt_current(&mut self) -> Result<ThreadId, SchedulerError> {
199:         let current = self.current.ok_or(SchedulerError::NoCurrentThread)?;
200:         let slot = self.thread_slot_mut(current)?;
201:         if slot.state != Some(ThreadState::Running) {
202:             return Err(SchedulerError::InvalidTransition);
203:         }
204:         slot.state = Some(ThreadState::Ready);
205:         self.current = None;
206:         self.select_ready().ok_or(SchedulerError::InvalidTransition)
207:     }
208: 
209:     /// Exit the running thread and dispatch another ready thread, if one exists.
210:     /// The exited slot remains unavailable until explicitly reaped.
211:     pub fn exit_current(&mut self) -> Result<Option<ThreadId>, SchedulerError> {
212:         let current = self.current.ok_or(SchedulerError::NoCurrentThread)?;
213:         let slot = self.thread_slot_mut(current)?;
214:         if slot.state != Some(ThreadState::Running) {
215:             return Err(SchedulerError::InvalidTransition);
216:         }
217:         slot.state = Some(ThreadState::Exited);
218:         self.current = None;
219:         Ok(self.select_ready())
220:     }
221: 
222:     /// Release an exited thread slot so it can be reused with a new ID.
223:     pub fn reap(&mut self, thread: ThreadId) -> Result<(), SchedulerError> {
224:         let slot = self.thread_slot_mut(thread)?;
225:         if slot.state != Some(ThreadState::Exited) {
226:             return Err(SchedulerError::ThreadNotExited);
227:         }
228:         slot.state = None;
229:         Ok(())
230:     }
231: 
232:     /// Create an empty bounded wait queue.
233:     pub fn create_wait_queue(&mut self) -> Result<WaitQueueId, SchedulerError> {
234:         let mut generation_exhausted = false;
235:         for (index, queue) in self.queues.iter_mut().enumerate() {
236:             if queue.active {
237:                 continue;
238:             }
239:             let Some(generation) = queue.generation.checked_add(1) else {
240:                 generation_exhausted = true;
241:                 continue;
242:             };
243:             queue.generation = generation;
244:             queue.active = true;
245:             queue.len = 0;
246:             queue.waiters.fill(None);
247:             return Ok(WaitQueueId {
248:                 slot: index,
249:                 generation,
250:             });
251:         }
252:         if generation_exhausted {
253:             Err(SchedulerError::GenerationExhausted)
254:         } else {
255:             Err(SchedulerError::WaitQueueCapacityFull)
256:         }
257:     }
258: 
259:     /// Destroy an empty queue. A queue with sleepers cannot be destroyed.
260:     pub fn destroy_wait_queue(&mut self, queue: WaitQueueId) -> Result<(), SchedulerError> {
261:         let queue = self.queue_slot_mut(queue)?;
262:         if queue.len != 0 {
263:             return Err(SchedulerError::WaitQueueNotEmpty);
264:         }
265:         queue.active = false;
266:         Ok(())
267:     }
268: 
269:     /// Block the running thread on a queue, then dispatch another ready thread.
270:     /// A full or invalid queue leaves the current thread running.
271:     pub fn sleep_current(
272:         &mut self,
273:         queue_id: WaitQueueId,
274:     ) -> Result<Option<ThreadId>, SchedulerError> {
275:         let queue = self.queue_slot(queue_id)?;
276:         if queue.len == WAITERS {
277:             return Err(SchedulerError::WaiterCapacityFull);
278:         }
279:         let current = self.current.ok_or(SchedulerError::NoCurrentThread)?;
280:         let thread = self.thread_slot(current)?;
281:         if thread.state != Some(ThreadState::Running) {
282:             return Err(SchedulerError::InvalidTransition);
283:         }
284: 
285:         let queue = &mut self.queues[queue_id.slot];
286:         queue.waiters[queue.len] = Some(current);
287:         queue.len += 1;
288:         self.threads[current.slot].state = Some(ThreadState::Blocked(queue_id));
289:         self.current = None;
290:         Ok(self.select_ready())
291:     }
292: 
293:     /// Wake the oldest waiter, making it ready but not preempting the current
294:     /// thread. Returns `None` when the valid queue has no waiters.
295:     pub fn wake_one(&mut self, queue_id: WaitQueueId) -> Result<Option<ThreadId>, SchedulerError> {
296:         let queue = self.queue_slot(queue_id)?;
297:         if queue.len == 0 {
298:             return Ok(None);
299:         }
300:         let thread_id = queue.waiters[0].ok_or(SchedulerError::InvalidTransition)?;
301:         let thread = self.thread_slot(thread_id)?;
302:         if thread.state != Some(ThreadState::Blocked(queue_id)) {
303:             return Err(SchedulerError::InvalidTransition);
304:         }
305: 
306:         let queue = &mut self.queues[queue_id.slot];
307:         for index in 1..queue.len {
308:             queue.waiters[index - 1] = queue.waiters[index];
309:         }
310:         debug_assert!(queue.len > 0);
311:         queue.len -= 1;
312:         queue.waiters[queue.len] = None;
313:         self.threads[thread_id.slot].state = Some(ThreadState::Ready);
314:         Ok(Some(thread_id))
315:     }

317:     /// Wake every waiter in FIFO order, making them ready without preemption.
318:     pub fn wake_all(&mut self, queue_id: WaitQueueId) -> Result<usize, SchedulerError> {
319:         let queue = self.queue_slot(queue_id)?;
320:         let count = queue.len;
321: 
322:         for waiter in queue.waiters[..count].iter().flatten() {
323:             let thread = self.thread_slot(*waiter)?;
324:             if thread.state != Some(ThreadState::Blocked(queue_id)) {
325:                 return Err(SchedulerError::InvalidTransition);
326:             }
327:         }
328: 
329:         for waiter in self.queues[queue_id.slot].waiters[..count].iter().flatten() {
330:             self.threads[waiter.slot].state = Some(ThreadState::Ready);
331:         }
332:         let queue = &mut self.queues[queue_id.slot];
333:         queue.waiters.fill(None);
334:         queue.len = 0;
335:         Ok(count)
336:     }
337: 
338:     fn select_ready(&mut self) -> Option<ThreadId> {
339:         if THREADS == 0 {
340:             return None;
341:         }
342:         for offset in 0..THREADS {
343:             let index = (self.next_ready + offset) % THREADS;
344:             if self.threads[index].state == Some(ThreadState::Ready) {
345:                 let thread = ThreadId {
346:                     slot: index,
347:                     generation: self.threads[index].generation,
348:                 };
349:                 self.threads[index].state = Some(ThreadState::Running);
350:                 self.current = Some(thread);
351:                 self.next_ready = (index + 1) % THREADS;
352:                 return Some(thread);
353:             }
354:         }
355:         None
356:     }
357: 
358:     fn thread_slot(&self, thread: ThreadId) -> Result<&ThreadSlot, SchedulerError> {
359:         self.threads
360:             .get(thread.slot)
361:             .filter(|slot| slot.generation == thread.generation && slot.state.is_some())
362:             .ok_or(SchedulerError::InvalidThread)
363:     }
364: 
365:     fn thread_slot_mut(&mut self, thread: ThreadId) -> Result<&mut ThreadSlot, SchedulerError> {
366:         self.threads
367:             .get_mut(thread.slot)
368:             .filter(|slot| slot.generation == thread.generation && slot.state.is_some())
369:             .ok_or(SchedulerError::InvalidThread)
370:     }
371: 
372:     fn queue_slot(&self, queue: WaitQueueId) -> Result<&WaitQueue<WAITERS>, SchedulerError> {
373:         self.queues
374:             .get(queue.slot)
375:             .filter(|slot| slot.active && slot.generation == queue.generation)
376:             .ok_or(SchedulerError::InvalidWaitQueue)
377:     }
378: 
379:     fn queue_slot_mut(
380:         &mut self,
381:         queue: WaitQueueId,
382:     ) -> Result<&mut WaitQueue<WAITERS>, SchedulerError> {
383:         self.queues
384:             .get_mut(queue.slot)
385:             .filter(|slot| slot.active && slot.generation == queue.generation)
386:             .ok_or(SchedulerError::InvalidWaitQueue)
387:     }
388: }
389: 
390: impl<const THREADS: usize, const QUEUES: usize, const WAITERS: usize> Default
391:     for Scheduler<THREADS, QUEUES, WAITERS>
392: {
393:     fn default() -> Self {
394:         Self::new()
395:     }
396: }
