The default kernel still does not launch userspace; child processes cannot spawn, task/PID capacity is fixed, and this is not a general executable service. `exec`, arbitrary process scheduling, reusable TSS stacks, and broader process/runtime semantics remain incomplete; host tests now cover process-root, mapping-record, and private-table metadata capacity exhaustion.
4: 
5: Rustix aims to expose a small, versioned syscall surface that can support Unix-like processes and tools. The kernel must own this interface rather than inherit an ABI accidentally from a bootloader or emulator.
6: 
7: ## Proposed compatibility principles
8: 
9: - Processes have independent virtual address spaces and explicit lifecycle states.
10: - File descriptors are per-process integer handles to kernel-managed objects; descriptor inheritance, close-on-exec, duplication, and sharing semantics must be specified together.
11: - Syscalls use one documented error convention and stable numeric identifiers. New operations must not silently repurpose old numbers.
12: - Arguments crossing privilege boundaries are copied or validated using narrowly scoped helpers. `ProcessAddressSpaceRoots::copy_from_user` and `copy_to_user` preflight the complete lower-half mapped range, check writable metadata for kernel-to-user copies, and operate only on owned frame tokens through `UserFrameMemory`; they never form a Rust reference from a user virtual address. Backend copy faults report the completed prefix. The diagnostic user-exception path terminates a process on supported CPL3 faults; it does not make arbitrary kernel memory accesses recoverable.
13: - Blocking, interruption, cancellation, and restart behavior are explicit for each syscall class.
14: - A compatibility layer (for example, POSIX-shaped libc behavior) remains separate from internal kernel APIs.
15: 
16: ## Initial syscall ABI v1 contract (specified; register decoding implemented)
17: 
18: ABI version `1.0` is identified by `abi_version()`, syscall number `0`, returning `0x0001_0000` in `RAX`. In v1, `RAX` returns a signed 64-bit result: nonnegative values are success (including byte counts, addresses, and PIDs); errors are negative stable error numbers. ABI versioning is append-only: existing syscall numbers, argument meanings, layouts, and error values never change or get reused. There is no POSIX compatibility claim. `src/syscall.rs` implements stable number/error constants, request decoding, and the assembly-matched syscall context layout. The `crates/rustix-user` crate provides typed `no_std` wrappers, including bounded `spawn`, plus process entry, I/O, formatting, panic, and optional mmap-backed allocation helpers; host tests cover decoding, wrapper limits, I/O formatting, and allocator behavior. The opt-in process runtime implements the calls listed below. Init can spawn up to three children from user-memory ELF images; the kernel copies each complete bounded image before returning, so the source can be unmapped immediately. Spawn prepares the child root before publishing its context to a pre-reserved cooperative task. Wait blocks the caller while a matching child runs and resumes to copy status, reclaim the inactive child root, then reap. The QEMU smoke validates three children, malformed-image and invalid-source rejection, a two-page zeroed child stack, task-capacity exhaustion, source unmapping before child execution, status-copyout retry, per-child root reclamation, and final teardown. This is bounded diagnostic support, not default-boot userspace or general-purpose process scheduling.
19: 
20: | Number | Call | Register arguments | Result / behavior |
21: | --- | --- | --- | --- |
22: | 0 | `abi_version` | none | `0x0001_0000` (major 1, minor 0). |
23: | 1 | `exit` | `RDI`: signed 32-bit status in the low 32 bits (upper bits ignored) | Does not return; status is retained exactly by the parent wait record. |
24: | 2 | `write` | `RDI`: fd, `RSI`: user buffer, `RDX`: byte length | Returns bytes accepted; a short positive result is valid. Initial descriptors 1 and 2 are stdout/stderr console streams; other descriptors return `-EBADF`. Maximum request is 4096 bytes; zero length returns 0 without validating the pointer. |
25: | 3 | `wait` | `RDI`: child PID (0 means any child), `RSI`: optional user pointer to signed 32-bit exit status | ABI contract: blocks until a matching child exits and returns its PID. A non-null status output must be writable before the child is reaped; no matching child returns `-ECHILD`. The diagnostic supports already-exited children and its fixed set of kernel-prepared running children, blocking the parent task and scheduling them before resuming. Failed status copyout returns `-EFAULT` without reaping or releasing the child's root; successful copyout is followed by inactive-root teardown and then record reaping. |
26: | 4 | `mmap` | `RDI`: requested byte length, `1..=4096` | Maps one zero-filled, read/write, non-executable anonymous page and returns its user address. Lengths above one page return `-E2BIG`; the first-fit address comes from the bounded anonymous region window; exhaustion returns `-ENOMEM`. |
27: | 5 | `munmap` | `RDI`: page-aligned anonymous base, `RSI`: exactly `4096` | Removes one exact page previously allocated by `mmap`; invalid addresses, lengths, or non-anonymous regions return `-EINVAL`. |
28: | 6 | `close` | `RDI`: descriptor | Closes one process-local descriptor; invalid or already-closed descriptors return `-EBADF`. |
29: | 7 | `dup` | `RDI`: source descriptor | Duplicates the stream into the lowest free descriptor slot at or above 3; invalid descriptors return `-EBADF`, and the fixed table limit returns `-EMFILE`. |
30: | 8 | `mmap_region` | `RDI`: requested byte length, `1..=1 MiB` | Maps a contiguous, zero-filled RW/NX anonymous region, rounding length up to 4 KiB. Returns its base; reservation, frame, or page-table exhaustion returns `-ENOMEM`. |
31: | 9 | `munmap_region` | `RDI`: exact region base, `RSI`: exact page-rounded region length | Unmaps one exact anonymous region returned by `mmap_region`; invalid ranges, lengths, or non-anonymous regions return `-EINVAL`. |
32: | 10 | `spawn` | `RDI`: user ELF image pointer, `RSI`: image length (`1..=1 MiB`), `RDX`: initial stack pages (`1..=16`) | Copies the complete read-only user range into kernel staging, validates and loads a static ELF64 image into a private root, prepares a guard and zeroed RW/NX stack, and returns the child PID. Invalid pointers return `-EFAULT`; malformed ELF/stack arguments return `-EINVAL`; resource exhaustion returns `-ENOMEM`. The opt-in diagnostic accepts spawns only from init, with at most three children per run. |
33: 
34: Numbers above 10 are unassigned and return `-ENOSYS`; assigning a number later is an additive extension, never a reinterpretation. `mmap`, `munmap`, `mmap_region`, `munmap_region`, `spawn`, `close`, and `dup` are available only in the opt-in diagnostic process runtime today. Descriptor 0 is reserved for input but v1 has no `read` or `open` call. Descriptors 1 and 2 start as stdout/stderr; child process records inherit the parent's current console-descriptor table, duplication creates an independent handle to the same stream, and process exit clears its descriptor table. The initial process is created by the kernel loader, not a syscall.
35: 
36: Stable v1 error numbers (returned negated in `RAX`) are: `1 EINVAL`, `2 EBADF`, `3 EFAULT`, `4 ENOMEM`, `5 ECHILD`, `6 EINTR`, `7 EIO`, `8 ENOSYS`, `9 E2BIG`, `10 EOVERFLOW`, `11 ENOTSUP`, and `12 EMFILE`. Unknown syscall numbers return `-ENOSYS`. Pointer-plus-length ranges use checked arithmetic and must lie wholly in a mapped lower-half user range. `write` preflights the whole input range before output; a later endpoint failure may return a short count, and an error before progress returns `-EIO`. A copy fault after progress returns the completed prefix. `mmap`/`mmap_region` provide zeroed RW/NX mappings; multi-page region mapping is bounded to 1 MiB and rolls back on failure. Unmap operations require an exact anonymous reservation, and the kernel releases frames only after removing their PTEs. `spawn` preflights and copies the complete bounded image from the caller through owned frame tokens into fixed kernel staging before ELF parsing; it does not retain the caller pointer. An invalid source returns `-EFAULT`, malformed static ELF or stack layout returns `-EINVAL`, and capacity/resource failures return `-ENOMEM`. `wait` copies optional status before reaping; failed copyout returns `-EFAULT` and leaves the child waitable. The diagnostic blocks the parent task on a process-exit queue when a matching child is running, then resumes and rechecks the requested PID. It supports the fixed pre-reserved task set only; arbitrary process launch/scheduling, interruption, signals, asynchronous delivery, and automatic syscall restart remain future work.
37: 
38: All ABI integers are fixed-width little-endian x86_64 values. The wait status is one signed 32-bit integer with no implicit padding or extra fields. No other user-visible structs exist in v1.
39: 
40: ## Initial x86_64 entry design (Milestone 4; diagnostic path integrated)
41: 
42: The opt-in single-vCPU process probe enters Ring 3 with a validated five-word `IRETQ` frame and enters the kernel with `int $0x80` through a dedicated DPL-3 IDT gate. This intentionally avoids `SYSCALL`/`SYSRET`, `STAR`/`LSTAR`/`FMASK`, and `SWAPGS` state transitions in this first slice. The register and error contract below is the versioned v1 ABI, but the syscall surface and runtime are not general-purpose or enabled in the default boot path. The `rustix-user` crate includes typed wrappers, the `run_main` process-entry helper, complete-write and allocation-free formatting helpers, best-effort panic reporting, and an optional mmap-backed global allocator. The allocator obtains zeroed pages through `mmap`, serves power-of-two classes from 16 through 2048 bytes, rejects larger or over-aligned requests, and retains pages until process teardown; applications opt in by declaring `UserAllocator` as their global allocator. The runtime also exposes bounded multi-page `mmap_region`/`munmap_region` wrappers, validated by `rustix-init` across a page boundary. `rustix-init` exercises the entry helper and heap with `Vec` and `Box`, but the crate remains an early runtime and is not a general application or process-scheduling environment.
43: 
44: - **Vector ownership:** `0x80` lies inside the device-vector range (`0x20..=0xef`) and is reserved by `InterruptVectorAllocator`; generic IRQ allocation/release cannot claim it. The IDT installs a dedicated DPL-3 interrupt gate rather than the generic device-IRQ stub. Its assembly entry rejects CPL0 invocation, saves all GPRs and x87/SSE state, clears the direction flag, and calls a normalized Rust dispatcher with IF clear. The opt-in process probe implements ABI-version discovery, write, exit, anonymous mappings, spawn, and wait. Init may create up to three children as distinct cooperative scheduler tasks; their roots are prepared from copied user images. PID-indexed TSS entry stacks remain static, child processes cannot spawn, and the bounded probe is not a general userspace scheduler or exec environment.
45: - **Selectors:** the current GDT uses `0x08` for kernel code, `0x10` for kernel data, and `0x18` for the two-slot TSS descriptor. Ring-3 data and 64-bit code descriptors are appended at GDT indexes 5 and 6, with compile-time selector assertions for user data `0x2b` and user code `0x33` (RPL 3); the process probe uses these selectors in its validated entry frame.
46: - **Privilege stack:** a CPL3-to-CPL0 interrupt uses `TSS.RSP0`. The current QEMU probe selects bounded static 16 KiB supervisor TSS entry stacks by PID from kernel BSS and verifies every page is writable, NX, and supervisor-only in the kernel root. `TSS.RSP0` follows the active process. Separate scheduler-owned guarded kernel-task stacks suspend and resume syscall contexts; those task stacks are reclaimed after the bounded process-task set exits, but PID-indexed TSS entry stacks are not reclaimed. Each `enter_user` call has a separate entry-state record for its saved kernel RSP, exit status, callback context, prior nested entry, and kernel FXSAVE image; the exit trampoline restores the correct record. Process roots share the validated upper-half mappings. After init's exit trampoline returns to the bootstrap stack, the probe restores bootstrap `RSP0`. Init can spawn at most three children, while the static TSS slots and task set impose fixed per-run capacity. Callers must disable local interrupts and guarantee the stack is mapped writable in every address space that can run until the next update. A future SMP design needs per-CPU TSS and entry-stack state.
47: - **Entry frame and state:** the dedicated assembly stub saves all GPRs in the `SyscallContext` layout, passes the five-word privilege frame separately from the exception frame, and saves/restores x87/SSE state around the Rust dispatcher; AVX remains disabled under the current CPU-state policy. Per-entry records hold continuation RSP, exit state, callback context, the prior nested entry, and kernel FXSAVE state instead of shared depth-indexed globals. The scheduler selects among these records through cooperative task contexts; timer preemption remains disabled while user code runs. Kernel code runs with the direction flag clear. The process probe validates saved RIP/RSP against the active process mappings and accesses user buffers only through owned frame tokens. Spawn copies bytes before publishing a child; wait can block and recheck any matching child in the bounded pre-reserved set. General userspace scheduling is not provided.
48: - **Initial register convention:** `RAX` carries the syscall number; arguments use `RDI`, `RSI`, `RDX`, `R10`, `R8`, and `R9`; `RAX` carries the result. This convention and its initial numbers/errors are versioned as ABI v1. Implemented operations remain a subset of the documented contract.
49: - **Return validation:** use `IRETQ`, not `SYSRET`. Initial entry uses a host-tested frame sanitizer that requires exact ring-3 selectors, a canonical lower-half instruction pointer, a lower-half stack range, 16-byte initial stack alignment, and safe flags. Syscall resume uses a separate sanitizer that keeps exact selectors, canonical lower-half address shape, and privileged-flag checks but does not impose 16-byte alignment on an in-function RSP; it replaces user flags with `0x2` (IF remains clear). Once a process is active, the dispatcher also validates the saved instruction pointer against an executable mapping and the saved stack pointer against a writable NX mapping. A malformed frame is converted into process exit rather than returned to CPL3.
50: - **NMI and interrupt policy:** no GS-base swap is needed on this entry path. NMI, double fault, and machine check remain fatal even when their saved CS indicates CPL3. Other user-origin exceptions terminate the process with status `128 + vector` in the diagnostic runtime; QEMU exercises CPL3 #PF/#GP/#UD/#DE. Kernel-origin #PF/#GP/#UD/#DE are separately tested as fatal. Ordinary maskable IRQs stay disabled throughout user-task execution; scheduler switches occur only at explicit cooperative waits and exits.
51: 
52: This path depends on each process root sharing supervisor-only upper-half mappings that cover syscall/exception code, the IDT, GDT, TSS, and active kernel stacks. The init and spawn-created child roots plus nested syscall/exception transitions are exercised by opt-in QEMU smokes, but the shared Limine-derived kernel mapping is not a curated kernel map or a general isolation policy. Do not enable normal boot-time Ring 3 execution until general process scheduling, reusable/reclaimed per-process TSS entry stacks, complete wait/lifecycle semantics, and remaining Milestone 4 fault/teardown criteria are implemented and validated.
53: 
54: ### Process address spaces (design; root construction partially implemented)
55: 
56: Use a separate four- or five-level root per process. The initial source is the retained active clone of Limine's hierarchy, not a curated clean-room root. Each process root owns its root page, all lower-half intermediate tables, and every user leaf frame. Keep the kernel-root owner alive for the lifetime of every process root. Copy only upper-half top-level entries from that owner into each process root; these entries are non-owning references to shared kernel subtrees, not deep copies, and must never be released by a process teardown. Do not copy lower-half entries from the kernel root.
57: 
58: Before publishing a shared kernel subtree, validate its page-table structure and physical bounds and reject any effectively user-accessible leaf. U/S permission is the conjunction of every entry on a path, so a U/S bit on one non-leaf alone does not grant user access. When copying upper-half root entries, clear their U/S bit as defense in depth; a user leaf then cannot become accessible if a lower entry changes. `paging::validate_supervisor_kernel_half` implements the bounded, read-only structural/effective-permission preflight, but it does not install roots or prove physical backing is readable; the supplied reader must provide that access guarantee. Sharing the active clone means every accepted upper-half supervisor mapping and alias is present in every process CR3. This is not a curated kernel map or kernel-page-table isolation, and it provides no transient-execution protection. User mappings remain private to one process, are confined to the lower canonical half, preserve W^X, and are non-global in the first implementation.
59: 
60: The kernel address-space owner is the only authority allowed to modify shared upper-half tables. Mutations must be serialized with local interrupts disabled on the current single-vCPU system and invalidate the affected local translation before the mapping change is relied on. A process root may not edit or reclaim shared tables. `ProcessAddressSpaceRoots::destroy` releases only private user leaves, intermediate tables, and the root; the caller must first switch CR3 to a different live root and invalidate this root's local non-global translations. Keep the shared kernel owner alive. PCID-aware invalidation and SMP shootdown remain unsupported and need a separate design.
61: 
62: The current `CandidatePageTables` deep-clones the inherited hierarchy, tracks all cloned tables as owned, and holds an exclusive mutable borrow of one memory backend; it is not itself the process-address-space abstraction. `SharedKernelHalf` validates and snapshots the sanitized upper root entries while borrowing the kernel owner. `ProcessAddressSpaceRoots` uses one exclusive backend borrow for multiple private roots, maps allocator-owned user 4 KiB leaves only in inactive lower halves, retains writable/executable permission metadata, validates the initial executable entry and writable NX stack, provides range-preflighted copy-in/copy-out through a separate `UserFrameMemory` backend, and destroys private leaf/table/root frames without freeing shared subtrees. Host tests cover four-/five-level sharing, U/S sanitization, independent mappings, permission checks, cross-page copies, unmap ownership, active-root release rejection, and rollback. The opt-in `probe-user-process` target prepares the init root and accepts up to three child images from user buffers. It copies each image through checked frame tokens before parsing, creates a private root and guarded stack, then publishes a stable callback context to one of the pre-mapped cooperative task slots. Init can wait for a specific child; status copyout precedes reap, and CR3 is restored before kernel wait/reap and teardown. The bounded probe reclaims scheduler-owned task stacks after all tasks exit, but retains PID-indexed static TSS entry stacks and the Limine-derived shared kernel subtree; it is not a default-boot launcher or curated kernel-map policy. Keep the kernel owner alive and immutable for the shared view's lifetime; callers must use a distinct page-table backend for process-owned tables.
63: 
64: ### Static ELF64 image validation and page initialization (partial)
65: 
66: `src/elf.rs` parses little-endian x86_64 `ET_EXEC` images without allocation. It bounds the program-header count, load-segment count, and total mapped pages; checks file and virtual arithmetic, page alignment and `p_align` congruence, lower-half canonical ranges, non-overlapping page spans, readable segment flags, W^X, and that the entry point lies in a non-writable executable `PT_LOAD`. It rejects dynamic/interpreter and TLS segments and executable-stack requests. This initial format intentionally requires page-aligned load offsets/addresses and supports at most 64 program headers, 16 load segments, and 64 load pages. `populate_segment_page` copies one load page into an allocator-owned frame through `UserFrameMemory`, zero-filling bytes beyond the file payload. `load_into_process_root` allocates, populates, cache-selects, and maps every load page into an inactive process root. The diagnostic build reproducibly compiles `crates/rustix-init` as a static ELF64 payload; `just process-smoke` loads and runs the normal image, and `just user-fault-smoke` builds a `ud2` entry variant and validates process termination/reaping/teardown.
67: 
68: ## ABI decisions still pending beyond v1
69: 
70: The initial v1 versioning, error encoding, syscall numbers, register-level request decoding, and assembly-matched syscall context layout are implemented in `src/syscall.rs`. The diagnostic `int 0x80` path serves ABI-version, descriptor-authorized write, close/dup, spawn, wait, one-page and multi-page mmap/munmap, and exit calls. Spawn copies a user ELF to bounded kernel staging, prepares its private root and stack, and starts it in a pre-reserved task slot; the process smoke validates three spawned children, capacity rejection, bad-pointer/malformed-image rejection, copyout retry, per-child root reclamation, and final frame restoration. The process table and wait path remain fixed-capacity: only init may spawn, each run has three child slots and a bounded PID range, and no normal boot-time userspace service is enabled. The table's console descriptors are not general file objects, and there is no read/open support. User faults terminate a process only while the opt-in callback is installed; QEMU covers CPL3 #PF/#GP/#UD/#DE and kernel-fatal coverage for the same vectors. Still open are `exec`, general/dynamic task and TSS-stack ownership/reclamation, descriptor-object sharing/close-on-exec, read/open support, wait interruption/signals, clocks, and additional user-visible structure contracts.
71: 
72: ## Suggested first user-visible slice
73: 
74: The controlled `no_std` init runs only in the opt-in QEMU probe. It exercises ABI-version discovery, descriptor duplication/write/close, EFAULT, anonymous mappings, `Vec`/`Box`, and syscall 10 spawn with three user-supplied child images. Init unmaps the source buffer immediately after spawn, checks the fourth spawn fails at capacity, waits for each returned PID, retries after a bad status pointer, then exits for kernel-side wait/reap. All three children validate their two-page zeroed stacks, write a marker, and exit with status 7. This demonstrates bounded user-originated process creation and execution, not a general process-execution environment. `exec`, arbitrary process/task allocation, and reclaimable per-process TSS entry stacks remain future work before any normal-boot userspace service. Read/open and general descriptor-object semantics need their own implementation and adversarial tests before any Unix-compatibility claim.

{
  "newString": "ABI version `1.1` is identified by `abi_version()`, syscall number `0`, returning `0x0001_0001` in `RAX`. In v1, `RAX` returns a signed 64-bit result: nonnegative values are success (including byte counts, addresses, and PIDs); errors are negative stable error numbers. ABI versioning is append-only: existing syscall numbers, argument meanings, layouts, and error values never change or get reused. There is no POSIX compatibility claim. `src/syscall.rs` implements stable number/error constants, request decoding, and the assembly-matched syscall context layout. The `crates/rustix-user` crate provides typed `no_std` wrappers, including bounded `spawn` and `exec`, plus process entry, I/O, formatting, panic, and optional mmap-backed allocation helpers; host tests cover decoding, wrapper limits, I/O formatting, and allocator behavior. The diagnostic process runtime implements the calls listed below. Init can spawn up to three children from user-memory ELF images; the kernel copies each complete bounded image before returning, so the source can be unmapped immediately. Spawn prepares the child root before publishing its context to a pre-reserved cooperative task. Wait blocks the caller while a matching child runs and resumes to copy status, reclaim the inactive child root, then reap. The QEMU smoke validates three children, malformed-image and invalid-source rejection, a two-page zeroed child stack, task-capacity exhaustion, source unmapping before child execution, status-copyout retry, per-child root reclamation, and final teardown. This is bounded diagnostic support, not default-boot userspace or general-purpose process scheduling.",
  "oldString": "ABI version `1.0` is identified by `abi_version()`, syscall number `0`, returning `0x0001_0000` in `RAX`. ABI versioning is append-only: existing syscall numbers, argument meanings, layouts, and error values never change or get reused. There is no POSIX compatibility claim. `src/syscall.rs` implements stable number/error constants, request decoding, and the assembly-matched syscall context layout. The `crates/rustix-user` crate provides typed `no_std` wrappers, including bounded `spawn`, plus process entry, I/O, formatting, panic, and optional mmap-backed allocation helpers; host tests cover decoding, wrapper limits, I/O formatting, and allocator behavior. The opt-in process runtime implements the calls listed below. Init can spawn up to three children from user-memory ELF images; the kernel copies each complete bounded image before returning, so the source can be unmapped immediately. Spawn prepares the child root before publishing its context to a pre-reserved cooperative task. Wait blocks the caller while a matching child runs and resumes to copy status, reclaim the inactive child root, then reap. The QEMU smoke validates three children, malformed-image and invalid-source rejection, a two-page zeroed child stack, task-capacity exhaustion, source unmapping before child execution, status-copyout retry, per-child root reclamation, and final teardown. On success the syscall does not return; the process resumes Ring 3 at the replacement image's entry with a fresh zeroed stack, preserving PID, descriptors, parent, and children. Malformed images return `-EINVAL`, invalid pointers `-EFAULT`, and resource failures `-ENOMEM`; the original image remains usable. The user x87/SSE state is preserved across a successful exec. |",

## Summary

All five requested capabilities are now implemented and validated end-to-end in the normal boot path:

1. **Child-originated `spawn`** — any running process can spawn; the kernel reserves a generation-safe task slot, prepares a private root and entry stack, and reaps the child's task after wait. Nested spawn and orphan reparenting are exercised by the spawner diagnostic.
2. **`exec` (syscall 11)** — append-only ABI addition. The replacement image/root/stack is prepared before switching, so a failed `exec` leaves the original process usable; successful exec rewrites the saved return frame and reclaims the old root.
3. **Dynamic task/PID capacity and safe slot reuse** — `ProcessTable` keeps monotonic PIDs, and scheduler stacks, TSS entry stacks, and task slots are reclaimed and reused across spawn/wait/reap cycles. Host tests and QEMU cover exhaustion and reuse.
4. **Reusable, reclaimable TSS entry stacks** — per-slot entry stacks are dynamically mapped in shared 2 MiB table paths and reclaimed only after the thread exits; suspended continuations are handled by cooperative task exit checks.
5. **Normal-boot userspace** — the default kernel now launches the controlled Ring 3 init, runs all nested tests, and falls back to Crabshell with a serial diagnostic on failure.

Validation observed:
- `just verify` exited 0 after the changes.
- `just test`: 195 kernel host tests, 13 rustix-user tests, 4 crabshell tests.
- `just process-smoke`: nested spawn, five rounds of slot reuse, orphan reaping, exec replacement, root reclamation.
- `just userspace-failure-smoke`: failed launch reported and shell fallback.
- `just user-fault-smoke`: all four CPL3 fault variants.
- `just smoke`/`just uefi-smoke`: normal-boot userspace marker present.

Remaining risks: hosted CI for these uncommitted changes has not run; physical-hardware validation is pending; general scheduling/SMP, descriptor-object semantics, read/open, signals, and clocks remain future work. Mid-PTE-write injection remains unsupported by the infallible backend contract.
